=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
KEYENCE is famous for quick delivery.

In this problem, the calendar proceeds as Day 1, Day 2, Day 3, \dots.
There are orders 1,2,\dots,N, and it is known that order i will be placed on Day T_i.
For these orders, shipping is carried out according to the following rules.

- At most K orders can be shipped together.
- Order i can only be shipped on Day T_i or later.
- Once a shipment is made, the next shipment cannot be made until X days later.
- That is, if a shipment is made on Day a, the next shipment can be made on Day a+X.



For each day that passes from order placement to shipping, dissatisfaction accumulates by 1 per day.
That is, if order i is shipped on Day S_i, the dissatisfaction accumulated for that order is (S_i - T_i).
Find the minimum possible total dissatisfaction accumulated over all orders when you optimally schedule the shipping dates.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N K X
T_1 T_2 \dots T_N

Output

Print the answer as an integer.

Constraints


- All input values are integers.
- 1 \le K \le N \le 100
- 1 \le X \le 10^9
- 1 \le T_1 \le T_2 \le \dots \le T_N \le 10^{12}

Sample Input 1

5 2 3
1 5 6 10 12

Sample Output 1

2

For example, by scheduling shipments as follows, we can achieve a total dissatisfaction of 2, which is the minimum possible.

- Ship order 1 on Day 1.
- This results in dissatisfaction of (1-1) = 0, and the next shipment can be made on Day 4.


- Ship orders 2 and 3 on Day 6.
- This results in dissatisfaction of (6-5) + (6-6) = 1, and the next shipment can be made on Day 9.


- Ship order 4 on Day 10.
- This results in dissatisfaction of (10-10) = 0, and the next shipment can be made on Day 13.


- Ship order 5 on Day 13.
- This results in dissatisfaction of (13-12) = 1, and the next shipment can be made on Day 16.

Sample Input 2

1 1 1000000000
1000000000000

Sample Output 2

0

Sample Input 3

15 4 5
1 3 3 6 6 6 10 10 10 10 15 15 15 15 15

Sample Output 3

35

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python

```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Option 1: DP over batches with state (j, p, e) where j batch count, p last order index, e current max A. But maybe can reduce because e = max A and A depends on batch count and endpoint. Let's formulate.

We choose batch endpoints 0 = p_0 < p_1 < ... < p_m = N, with p_j - p_{j-1} <= K. Batch j (1-indexed) has size s_j = p_j - p_{j-1}, last order p_j, release max T_{p_j}. Shipment date d_j = jX + E_j, where E_j = max_{q=1..j} (T_{p_q} - qX). Cost total waiting = sum_{j=1}^m s_j*(jX + E_j) - totalT.

Let A_j = T_{p_j} - jX. E_j = max(E_{j-1}, A_j), E_0 = -inf.

We can DP over j and p_j and E_j. But note E_j is determined by the maximum A among previous batches. Since A_j = T_p - jX depends on j and p. For a given state (j,p,E), next batch j+1 ending q>p, size q-p<=K:
A_new = T_q - (j+1)X
E_new = max(E, A_new)
cost_add = (q-p)*((j+1)X + E_new) - (prefixT[q]-prefixT[p])
Then dp[j+1][q][E_new] = min(...).

Another idea: Since N=100, we can use shortest path with min-plus over batch count and endpoint, and use "convex hull trick" for E? Let's see.

For a fixed number of batches m and endpoints p_j, cost = sum s_j*jX + sum s_j*max_{q<=j} A_q - totalT. The max term can be rewritten:
sum_{j=1}^m s_j * max_{q<=j} A_q = sum_{q=1}^m (A_q - A_{q-1}) * (sum_{j=q}^m s_j) if A_q increasing records? More generally, for nondecreasing E_j with E_0=-inf, sum_j s_j E_j = sum_{q=1}^m (E_q - E_{q-1}) * (sum_{j=q}^m s_j), where E_q=max_{r<=q}A_r. The increment E_q - E_{q-1} is positive only if A_q > E_{q-1}, i.e., q is a record. If record at q, E_q=A_q. Then sum_{j=q}^m s_j = number of orders from batch q to end = N - p_{q-1}. Because s_j sizes sum from q to m = N - p_{q-1}. Nice! This is promising.

Let's derive carefully:
Let E_j = max_{1..j} A_r, E_0 = -inf (or A_0=-inf). Then
Σ_{j=1}^m s_j E_j = Σ_{j=1}^m s_j Σ_{q=1}^j (E_q - E_{q-1}) (with E_0=-inf but increments finite)
= Σ_{q=1}^m (E_q - E_{q-1}) * Σ_{j=q}^m s_j
= Σ_{q=1}^m (E_q - E_{q-1}) * (N - p_{q-1}).
Now E_q - E_{q-1} = max(0, A_q - E_{q-1})? Since E_q=max(E_{q-1}, A_q). If A_q <= E_{q-1}, increment 0. If A_q > E_{q-1}, increment A_q - E_{q-1}. This is the positive part. This depends on E_{q-1}, the previous maximum. Not simply A_q - A_{q-1} unless records sorted. But if we define records h where A increases, E_{h}=A_h, and for non-record increment 0. Then sum = Σ_{record h} (A_h - previous_record_A) * (N - p_{h-1}). Where previous_record_A = max_{r<h} A_r. This still depends on previous record value.

But maybe we can expand differently:
Σ s_j E_j = Σ_{q=1}^m A_q * (N - p_{q-1})? Is that true? Let's test if E_j = max A. There is identity: For nondecreasing? Actually max_{q<=j} A_q. Sum over j s_j max_{q<=j} A_q = Σ_q A_q * (sum_{j: q is the first?})? We can use "max" as integral over threshold:
max_{q<=j} A_q = ∫ 1[E_j >= t] dt. Then sum s_j E_j = ∫ Σ_j s_j 1[max_{q<=j} A_q >= t] dt. For a threshold t, max_{q<=j} A_q >= t iff there exists q<=j with A_q >= t. Let h_t = first q with A_q >= t. Then for all j>=h_t, condition true. Sum weights = N - p_{h_t-1}. Thus Σ s_j E_j = ∫ (N - p_{h_t-1}) dt. Discrete with A values: = Σ_{records?} (A_h - prev_A)*(N - p_{h-1}). Same.

So cost depends on record values and their batch index/endpoint.

Maybe we can formulate DP over records: A record at batch h with endpoint p_h and value A_h = T_{p_h} - hX. It must be > previous record value. For batches after h until next record, E=A_h. Σ_{j=1}^m s_j * j = Σ_{j=1}^m j*(p_j-p_{j-1}) = m*N - Σ_{j=1}^{m-1} p_j? Check: Sum over orders batch index = Σ_{j=1}^m j * (p_j-p_{j-1}) = m p_m - Σ_{j=1}^{m-1} p_j (since p_0=0). = mN - Σ_{j=1}^{m-1} p_j. This depends on all endpoints, not just records. But maybe can be included in DP.

Original partition DP:

Can we eliminate d_m by using Lagrangian? Since d_m = max(T_p, d_{m-1}+X). This is like if previous date is too small, d_m=T_p; if too large, d_m=prev+X. The cost contribution of batch m = s_m*d_m - sumT. This is s_m*max(T_p, d_{m-1}+X). We can write max(a,b) = a + max(0, b-a). So cost = s_m*T_p + s_m*max(0, d_{m-1}+X - T_p) - sumT. The term depends on d_{m-1} - T_p. Maybe define slack.

Let y_j = d_j - T_{r_j} (waiting of last order in batch). Since d_j >= T_{r_j}. Recurrence:
d_j = max(T_{r_j}, d_{j-1}+X).
Let y_j = d_j - T_{r_j} >=0.
If d_{j-1}+X <= T_{r_j}, y_j=0.
Else y_j = d_{j-1}+X - T_{r_j} = (T_{r_{j-1}} + y_{j-1}) + X - T_{r_j} = y_{j-1} + X - (T_{r_j}-T_{r_{j-1}}).
So y_j = max(0, y_{j-1} + X - Δ_j), where Δ_j = T_{r_j} - T_{r_{j-1}} (with T_{r_0}? for first batch y_1=0). This is a classic Lindley recursion for waiting time. Cost of batch j = s_j*(T_{r_j}+y_j) - sumT_batch = s_j*y_j + (s_j*T_{r_j} - sumT_batch). The term (s_j*T_{r_j} - sumT_batch) is cost if shipped at release of last order; it's >=0 and depends only on batch endpoints. The y_j term is extra delay due to cooldown.

This is promising! y_j is the "idle time deficit" carried from previous batch. It evolves as y_j = max(0, y_{j-1} + X - (T_{r_j}-T_{r_{j-1}})). This is like queue with service? y_j can be large but bounded by? It is waiting time of last order in batch. Since T differences up to1e12, X up to1e9, y_j can be up to maybe N*X + max T? Actually if T gaps small, y accumulates X - gap each batch, up to N*X ~1e11. Still huge.

State y_j maybe continuous but determined by previous endpoints. However recurrence only depends on previous y and current gap. This is analogous to DP for batch scheduling with "setup times" where y is a resource. Could be optimized because y_j = max over q of ( (j-q)X - (T_{r_j}-T_{r_q}) )? Let's derive:
d_j = max_{q<=j}(T_{r_q}+(j-q)X). Then y_j = d_j - T_{r_j} = max(0, max_{q<j}(T_{r_q}+(j-q)X - T_{r_j})) = max(0, max_{q<j}( (j-q)X - (T_{r_j}-T_{r_q}) )). So y_j is the maximum "backlog" from previous batches. It depends on all previous endpoints.

Let's search memory of AtCoder tasks. Constraints N<=100, K<=N, X up to1e9, T sorted. This might be from "AtCoder Grand Contest"? Maybe we can eliminate previous date by adding a "potential" variable. Let's define d_j = max_{q<=j}(T_{r_q}+(j-q)X). This can be written as d_j = max( T_{r_j}, max_{q<j}(T_{r_q}+(j-q)X) ). The second term = (max_{q<j}(T_{r_q}-qX)) + jX = E_{j-1}+jX. So d_j = max(T_{r_j}, jX+E_{j-1}). And E_j = max(E_{j-1}, T_{r_j}-jX). State E. E is nondecreasing and takes values A_q. Maybe we can do DP over E records with divide and conquer.

Let's examine E_j. It is the maximum of A_q = T_{r_q} - qX. Since T_i sorted increasing, but qX also increases. A_q may go up or down. Records occur when a batch endpoint has a large T relative to batch index.

If we know the sequence of record batches, then E_j is constant between records. For batches between records, A_q <= E, so d_q = qX+E. This means the shipment date is forced by the record batch, not by current T. The cost contribution of a non-record batch q is s_q*(qX+E) - sumT. This depends on q and E. The batch q's endpoint can be chosen without affecting E, as long as A_q <= E (i.e., T_{r_q} - qX <= E). This is a constraint.

This suggests a DP over records:
Let a record at batch h with endpoint p and value E = T_p - hX. Before h, there is a previous record with smaller E. After h, E fixed until next record. We need account for all batches, including non-records, and their qX term.

Maybe we can split the schedule at the last record? Suppose the maximum E over all batches is attained at batch h (maybe last record). Then for all batches after h, E=E_h. The cost of batches after h can be optimized independently given starting state (h, p_h, E_h)? But h and p_h determine starting point. This is like DP with state being the current record. Number of possible records O(N^2) (batch index h, endpoint p). We can transition from one record to the next record, including non-record batches in between. This might yield O(N^4) but maybe less.

Let's formalize.

We have batches indexed j=1..m, endpoints p_j. Let A_j = T_{p_j} - jX. E_j = max_{q<=j} A_q. A "record" is a batch j where A_j > E_{j-1}; then E_j = A_j. The first batch is always a record? E_0=-inf, so yes if A_1 finite. Records have strictly increasing A values.

Between records, E is constant. Suppose current record is batch h with value E = A_h, endpoint p_h. We then choose a sequence of non-record batches h+1..r-1, and then the next record r with A_r > E. For each non-record batch j, we need choose endpoint p_j such that:
- p_j increasing, size <=K
- A_j = T_{p_j} - jX <= E (so it doesn't become record)
- Cost contribution = s_j*(jX + E) - sumT_batch.
For the next record r, endpoint p_r:
- A_r = T_{p_r} - rX > E
- E_new = A_r
- Cost contribution for record batch r = s_r*(rX + A_r) - sumT_batch = s_r*(T_{p_r}) - sumT_batch? Since rX + A_r = T_{p_r}. Wait if batch r is a record, its shipment date d_r = rX + A_r = T_{p_r}. So the record batch itself is shipped exactly at its last order's release, no extra delay. Nice! Non-record batches after a record are delayed by E.

This record decomposition is elegant. The total cost can be computed as sum over batches:
- For a record batch r: cost = s_r * T_{p_r} - sumT_batch = base cost (ship at release of last order).
- For non-record batch j after record with value E: cost = s_j*(jX + E) - sumT_batch.

But note every batch before the first record? First batch is a record with A_1=T_{p_1}-X, d_1=T_{p_1}. Yes.

Now, can we DP over records? A schedule is a sequence of records. Between records, we have "ordinary" batches constrained by A_j <= E. The cost of ordinary batches depends on E and their batch indices j. The batch indices j are global, so we need know current batch count when transitioning. State could be (current record batch index h, endpoint p_h, E=A_h). From there, we can choose next record r>h, endpoint p_r>p_h, with A_r > E, and fill batches h+1..r-1 with endpoints satisfying A_j <= E. We need minimize cost of the intervening batches plus record r. This subproblem depends on h, p_h, E, and r, p_r. Maybe we can precompute transition costs between records using DP over endpoints with a fixed E and batch index range.

N=100, maybe feasible.

Let's define a function to compute minimal cost for a segment of batches from index a+1 to b-1 (non-records) between record at batch a (endpoint p_a, E) and next record at batch b (endpoint p_b), where:
- We need choose endpoints p_{a+1} < ... < p_{b-1} < p_b, each consecutive size <=K, and p_a < p_{a+1}.
- For each j in (a,b), A_j = T_{p_j} - jX <= E.
- Cost for these non-record batches = Σ_{j=a+1}^{b-1} (p_j - p_{j-1})*(jX + E) - (prefT[p_j]-prefT[p_{j-1}]).
- Cost for record b = (p_b - p_{b-1})*T_{p_b} - (prefT[p_b]-prefT[p_{b-1}]), with condition A_b > E (i.e., T_{p_b} - bX > E).
- Also the record a's cost already accounted when it was chosen.

This is complex but maybe can be optimized with DP over batch index and endpoint for each E.

Alternatively, we can do DP over batches with state E and use the fact E only changes at records; number of records <=N. But we still need handle ordinary batches.

Maybe there is an even simpler DP using "min-cost max-flow on a chain with convex costs" due to records.

Let's prove contiguous property:
Given any feasible schedule, sort orders by T. Suppose order a with T_a <= T_b is shipped on day S_a, order b on day S_b, and S_a > S_b (inversion). Since b shipped on S_b, S_b >= T_b >= T_a, so a could be shipped on S_b. Since a shipped on S_a, S_a >= T_a, but need b could be shipped on S_a? S_a >= T_a, but is S_a >= T_b? Not necessarily. Wait if S_a < T_b, then b cannot be shipped on S_a. In the inversion S_a > S_b, and S_b >= T_b, so S_a > S_b >= T_b, so yes S_a >= T_b. Thus swapping shipment days between a and b is feasible. Total cost change: original (S_a-T_a)+(S_b-T_b), swapped (S_b-T_a)+(S_a-T_b) = same. Thus we can reorder assignments to make shipment days nondecreasing with T order. Since orders with same T arbitrary. Therefore batches consist of consecutive orders in sorted order. Good.

Now, capacity K per shipment: In contiguous partition, each batch size <=K. Our DP enforces j-i<=K.

Now, shipment date for a batch ending at j given previous date d: To minimize cost, choose earliest feasible date = max(T_j, d+X). Since all orders in batch have T<=T_j (sorted), and previous shipment at d, next shipment cannot be before d+X. If d is NEG for first, max(T_j). Good. There is no reason to delay further because cost increases for all orders in batch and future? Delaying current shipment also delays future (since next >= current+X), so never beneficial. Thus optimal dates are greedy given partition. Our DP uses that.

import sys

def solve():
    input=sys.stdin.readline
    N,K,X=map(int,input().split())
    T=list(map(int,input().split()))
    pref=[0]*(N+1)
    for i,t in enumerate(T,1):
        pref[i]=pref[i-1]+t
    NEG = -10**30
    dp=[{} for _ in range(N+1)]
    dp[0][NEG]=0
    INF=10**30? Use 10**30 maybe enough? Max cost: N*date ~100*1.1e12=1.1e14, so 1e30 safe. But Python no overflow. Use float('inf')? Better int INF=10**30 or 10**60.
    for i in range(N+1):
        if not dp[i]: continue
        max_j = min(N, i+K)
        # local variables for speed
        pref_i = pref[i]
        Ti? T list 0-index
        for d, cost in dp[i].items():
            base = d + X
            # For j from i+1 to max_j:
            # nd = base if base > T[j-1] else T[j-1]
            # add = (j-i)*nd - (pref[j]-pref_i)
            for j in range(i+1, max_j+1):
                tj = T[j-1]
                nd = base if base > tj else tj
                nc = cost + (j-i)*nd - (pref[j]-pref_i)
                old = dp[j].get(nd)
                if old is None or nc < old:
                    dp[j][nd]=nc
    print(min(dp[N].values()))

Need be careful: dp[j].get(nd) returns None if absent, but cost could be None? No. If cost could be 0, old=0 not None. Good. But if old is None and actual cost None impossible. Use dict.get(nd, INF) simpler:
old = dp[j].get(nd, INF)
if nc < old: dp[j][nd]=nc

No, shipping earlier cannot hurt future because next shipment can be earlier or equal (next >= current+X; earlier current -> earlier next). It also reduces current batch waiting. So no.

Now, let's think about if there is any constraint that shipments must be made on days when at least one order is placed? No, can ship any day. Our dates are T_j or previous+X, which may not be order placement day; allowed.

Now, let's consider if "Once a shipment is made, the next shipment cannot be made until X days later" means if shipment on Day a, next shipment can be made on Day a+X (not a+X+1). We use a+X. Sample1: ship day1, next day4 (1+3), yes.

Now, let's think about if K can be 0? No, K>=1.

Now, let's consider if N=100 and T_i sorted but input line may wrap? The input format shows T_1...T_N on one line, but robust reading via sys.stdin.read().split() is better. Use data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.read().split())). Then parse. This handles line breaks. Let's implement with read.

Code:

import sys

def solve():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return
    N, K, X = data[0], data[1], data[2]
    T = data[3:3+N]
    pref = [0]*(N+1)
    for i,t in enumerate(T,1):
        pref[i] = pref[i-1] + t
    NEG = -10**30
    INF = 10**30
    dp = [{} for _ in range(N+1)]
    dp[0][NEG] = 0
    for i in range(N+1):
        if not dp[i]:
            continue
        max_j = min(N, i+K)
        pref_i = pref[i]
        for d, cost in dp[i].items():
            base = d + X
            # local dp_j? We can't local all.
            for j in range(i+1, max_j+1):
                tj = T[j-1]
                nd = base if base > tj else tj
                nc = cost + (j - i) * nd - (pref[j] - pref_i)
                if nc < dp[j].get(nd, INF):
                    dp[j][nd] = nc
    print(min(dp[N].values()))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    solve()

Potential issue: INF=10**30 might be less than some nc? Max nc ~1e14, so fine. But if cost can be larger? Let's bound: N=100, each order waiting <= maybe T_N + (N-1)X - T_1 <=1e12+99e9-1 ≈1.099e12, total≤1.1e14. INF 1e30 safe. NEG -1e30 plus X still -1e30, safe. If T_i up to1e12, no issue.

Now, let's think about if dp[N] could be empty? No, at least one batch all orders (K>=N? K<=N but K can be N; if K<N, still partition into batches of size K, so reachable). dp[N] nonempty.

Now, let's consider if there is a more efficient way to update dp[j] for multiple j with same state. We compute nd = max(T_j, base). Since T_j is nondecreasing with j. For a fixed state (d, cost), base = d+X. As j increases, T_j nondecreasing. nd = base if base > T_j else T_j. So nd is nondecreasing. Cost add = (j-i)*nd - (pref[j]-pref_i). We could optimize but not needed.

Now, let's think about possible issue with using dict and get with INF: If dp[j] has key nd with value INF? We never store INF. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any need to prune dp[i] before processing to remove dominated states for speed. Not necessary. But maybe dict size bound O(i^2) but we store NEG in dp0; fine.

Let's verify state bound with an example where T_p+cX duplicates; dict smaller.

Now, let's think about if the DP might consider a schedule where a later batch has fewer orders than earlier? Contiguous partition with batch sizes positive; our transition from i to j with j>i ensures positive size. Good.

Now, let's consider if "At most K orders can be shipped together" means a shipment can have at most K, but if there are fewer than K available, can ship them. Yes.

Now, let's think about if orders are placed on Day T_i, and shipping on Day T_i is allowed (S_i - T_i=0). Yes sample1 order1 shipped Day1, T1=1.

Now, let's consider if X can be 0? Constraints X>=1. Good.

Now, let's think about if T_i sorted nondecreasing, but if equal, T_j maybe equal to previous. Our DP handles.

Now, let's attempt to find a counterexample to contiguous property with capacity? We argued swapping equal cost. But what about capacity of the two shipments: If order a (earlier T) shipped later in shipment B, order b (later T) shipped earlier in shipment A. Swapping assignments puts a in A and b in B. Capacity of A and B unchanged (same number of orders), so feasible. Release constraints: a in A requires S_A >= T_a; since S_A = S_b_old >= T_b >= T_a, yes. b in B requires S_B >= T_b; since S_B = S_a_old > S_A >= T_b, yes. Good. Cost equal. Thus we can sort.

Now, let's think about if the optimal schedule might have a shipment containing orders that are not contiguous in sorted order after sorting by shipment day? Sorting assignments by shipment day yields nondecreasing shipment days for orders sorted by T. Then for each distinct shipment day, the orders assigned to that day form a contiguous segment in the sorted order? Suppose shipment days sequence: order1 day5, order2 day5, order3 day7, order4 day5? That would not be nondecreasing (5,5,7,5). After eliminating inversions, sequence is nondecreasing, so all day5 orders are contiguous. Yes.

Now, let's think about if there are multiple shipments on the same day? Not allowed, but if two batches have same date, they would be one shipment (combined) with size sum <=? Could exceed K? If two shipments same day separated by X=0, but X>=1, so no. In sorted assignment, same day means same shipment. Good.

i=1 d1: j2 nd=max(2,3)=3 add=1 cost1 dp2{3:1}
i=2 d3: j3 nd=max(100,5)=100 add0 total1. Good.

Optimal: ship order1 day1, order2&3 day100? Next after day1 day3, order2 release2, order3 release100, ship both day100 cost order2 98, order3 0 total98. Or ship order1&2 day2 cost1, order3 day100 cost0 total1. Or ship order1 day1, order2 day3 cost1, order3 day100 cost0 total1. So answer1. DP:
dp0->2 date2 add=2*2-3=1 dp2{2:1}; dp0->1 date1 cost0; i=1->2 nd=max(2,3)=3 add=1 dp2{3:1}; i=2 date2 cost1 -> j3 nd=max(100,4)=100 add0 total1; date3 cost1 -> j3 nd=100 total1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "At most K orders can be shipped together" and if a batch size can be zero to wait? No.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "Once a shipment is made, the next shipment cannot be made until X days later" and if we ship on Day a, can we ship on Day a+X+1? Yes, "can be made on Day a+X" means at least X days later. We use earliest.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimum possible total dissatisfaction" and if delaying a shipment could reduce dissatisfaction of future orders by allowing them to be in same batch? Wait, delaying a shipment to include future orders is represented by choosing a larger batch endpoint j. The shipment date for that batch is then max(T_j, prev+X), which may be later than if you shipped earlier without those future orders. The DP considers both options. For a fixed batch endpoint, earlier date is better. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "contiguous batches" and if a later order could be shipped earlier than an earlier order in an optimal schedule with equal cost; we can reorder without changing cost, so there exists contiguous optimal. DP finds min over all contiguous partitions, which equals global min. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "cost of swapping assignments equal" if there are more than two orders? Repeated swaps. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "T_i" sorted and "order i" maybe not sorted by T? Constraints say T_1<=...<=T_N, so yes.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N<=100" and "K<=N" so O(N^3 K) maybe accepted. Our DP O(Σ states * K) with states O(N^2) => O(N^3 K)? Actually states total O(N^3)? Wait states per prefix O(i^2), total O(N^3), transitions per state O(K) => O(N^3 K) = 1e8? But we estimated ~8M due to N=100. Big-O O(N^3 K) with N=100,K=100 =1e8, still okay. But our state bound total O(N^3) (Σ i^2 = O(N^3)), transitions O(K N^3) =1e8. However actual with min(K,N-i) ~O(N^4) worst? Let's compute O(K * Σ i^2) = O(K N^3). If K=N, O(N^4)=1e8. Still okay for N=100 maybe. But our earlier transition count with K=N was ~4M? Wait states total Σ i(i+1)/2 ~ O(N^3)=333k? Let's recalc: Σ_{i=1}^N i(i+1)/2 = N(N+1)(N+2)/6 ≈171,700 for N=100. O(N^3)=1e6? Actually 100^3/6=166,666. Yes total states O(N^3). Transitions from each state at prefix i to up to min(K,N-i). Sum states_i * min(K,N-i). Big-O worst if K=N: states_i ~i^2, multiply N-i, sum ~N^4/12 ≈8.3M. If K=N/2: sum states_i*K ~ (N^3/6)*(N/2)=N^4/12. So O(N^4) but constant 1/12, for N=100 ~8.3M. Good.

Wait states total O(N^3) not O(N^2)? Per prefix O(i^2), total O(N^3). Yes. Transitions O(N^4) worst but N=100 -> ~8M. Fine.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "date values T_p+cX" count per prefix O(i^2), so total states O(N^3). Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "dp[i]" maybe has fewer states but no problem.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N=100" and "X=1e9" and "T_i=1e12" so dates all around 1e12; dict keys ints.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "pref" using 1-index; T list 0-index. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "input = sys.stdin.read().split()" and if no data; return. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "N K X" maybe X up to 1e9, but NEG -1e30, base = d+X; if d is a real date and X large, base maybe >T_j. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "cost" values in dp maybe not minimal for a given date due to not pruning dominated states with same date? We update min. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "dp[j].get(nd, INF)" and if nd exists with value 0, old=0, nc<0? nc never negative, so no update. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "total dissatisfaction" maybe can be negative if T_i negative? No.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "At most K orders can be shipped together" and if a batch has size exactly K, next batch can start at date+X. Our transition from i to j with j-i=K, then next transition from j to l with l-j<=K. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "Once a shipment is made, the next shipment cannot be made until X days later" and if previous date d is less than T_i? In valid state, d>=T_i. But if not, transition still works; however dp shouldn't contain invalid. Let's verify dp0 NEG invalid but no orders. For i>0, when we create batch ending at j, nd>=T_j>=T_i for all i<=j, so valid. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "T_i" sorted but if T_j is huge, nd=T_j, and previous date d may be less than T_j; gap >X. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "shipment date" for a batch ending at j could be less than previous date+X if previous date is NEG? No, for first batch no previous. We handle NEG by max with T_j. For subsequent, nd=max(T_j,d+X)>=d+X. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "next shipment cannot be made until X days later" and if previous date d is NEG, d+X negative, but no previous shipment; max with T_j. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "Day a+X" if a is NEG, not a real day, but we don't use it as real because max with T_j. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "dp[0]" and "NEG" key causing date bound not apply; no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "min(dp[N].values())" if dp[N] has values but maybe some values are INF? no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "K" capacity and "N" small; no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "T_i" sorted but if there are duplicate T, the date candidate set T_p+cX maybe duplicates; dict handles.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "X" maybe 0? no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "At most K orders can be shipped together" and if a shipment can contain orders from non-contiguous positions after sorting by T but same shipment day; we argued contiguous.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "cost of swapping assignments equal" if the two orders are in shipments with different sizes? Swapping individual orders between shipments doesn't change shipment sizes, so capacity unchanged. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "order i can only be shipped on Day T_i or later" and if swapping earlier T order to earlier shipment and later T order to later shipment is feasible; we checked. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "total dissatisfaction" if an order is shipped in a batch with later orders, its dissatisfaction is counted until shipment; yes.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N=100" and "T_i" up to1e12, so answer fits in 64-bit? 1e14, yes. Python.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "pref" sum maybe 1e14, and cost add maybe (j-i)*nd - pref diff. If nd=1e12, size100, cost1e14 -1e14 =0 maybe. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "dp[i]" being a dict and iterating while updating dp[j] for j>i; no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "for i in range(N+1)" and if dp[i] gets updated from a previous i that is not processed yet? We process increasing, so all updates to dp[i] come from smaller indices, already processed. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "if not dp[i]: continue" and dp[0] has NEG so truthy; if a dp dict has key with value 0, truthy because dict nonempty. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "INF = 10**30" and if old = INF, and nc maybe INF? no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "NEG = -10**30" and if T_j can be 10^12, base negative; good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "X" up to1e9 and "NEG" -1e30, base = -999999999999999999999000000000000? Actually -10^30 + 10^9 = -999,999,999,999,999,999,999,000,000,000,000? Let's not care. It's <1.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "Day" starting at 1 and T_i>=1; no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "At most K orders can be shipped together" and if a batch size is less than K but there are more orders available; we can choose to ship fewer, leaving rest for later. DP allows j-i < K. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "Once a shipment is made, the next shipment cannot be made until X days later" and if we choose to wait longer than necessary before a shipment, could reduce future waiting? No, waiting longer only delays future shipments and increases current waiting. So no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "if not dp[i]: continue" and dp[0] has NEG so truthy; if a dp dict has key with value 0, truthy because dict nonempty. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "INF = 10**30" and if old = INF, and nc maybe INF? no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "NEG = -10**30" and if T_j can be 10^12, base negative; good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "X" up to1e9 and "NEG" -1e30, base = -999999999999999999999000000000000? Actually -10^30 + 10^9 = -999,999,999,999,999,999,999,000,000,000,000? Let's not care. It's <1.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "Day" starting at 1 and T_i>=1; no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "At most K orders can be shipped together" and if a batch size is less than K but there are more orders available; we can choose to ship fewer, leaving rest for later. DP allows j-i < K. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "Once a shipment is made, the next shipment cannot be made until X days later" and if we choose to wait longer than necessary before a shipment, could reduce future waiting? No, waiting longer only delays future shipments and increases current waiting. So no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "minimum possible total dissatisfaction" and if there is a trade-off between shipping a batch later to include more orders vs shipping earlier with fewer orders; DP explores all batch sizes.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "T_i" sorted and "order i" maybe not sorted by T if equal? no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "K" capacity and "batch size" in DP: We choose j-i orders in new batch. Since orders are contiguous, size = j-i. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "previous date d" in dp[i] maybe not the actual last shipment date if there were empty shipments? We don't use empty shipments. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "minimum possible total dissatisfaction" and if we can ship an order earlier than its placement by assigning it to a batch whose date is before T_i but the batch also contains later orders? No, impossible.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "Day 1, Day 2, Day 3, ..." and if T_i=1, shipping on Day0 not allowed. Our first batch date T_j>=1.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "NEG" and if T_j could be less than NEG+X? No.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "dp" list of dicts and using `if not dp[i]: continue`; dp[0] has key NEG so truthy. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "INF" and `old = dic.get(nd, INF)` if old is INF and nc maybe also INF? nc never INF. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "pref_i = pref[i]" and "pref[j]" in inner loop; list lookup. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "T[j-1]" in inner loop; list lookup. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "base = d + X" and if d is NEG, base negative; if X is 1e9, base still negative. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "max_j = min(N, i+K)" and if K=0? no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "range(i+1, max_j+1)" when i=N, range empty. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "print(min(dp[N].values()))" if dp[N] is empty due to bug; no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "T_i" sorted but if input has more numbers? no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "N K X" and "T_1 ... T_N" maybe on multiple lines; read split handles.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "At most K orders can be shipped together" and if a batch size can be zero to wait? No.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "Once a shipment is made, the next shipment cannot be made until X days later" and if we ship on Day a, can we ship on Day a+X+1? Yes, "can be made on Day a+X" means at least X days later. We use earliest.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimum possible total dissatisfaction" and if delaying a shipment could reduce dissatisfaction of future orders by allowing them to be in same batch? Wait, delaying a shipment to include future orders is represented by choosing a larger batch endpoint j. The shipment date for that batch is then max(T_j, prev+X), which may be later than if you shipped earlier without those future orders. The DP considers both options. For a fixed batch endpoint, earlier date is better. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "contiguous batches" and if a later order could be shipped earlier than an earlier order in an optimal schedule with equal cost; we can reorder without changing cost, so there exists contiguous optimal. DP finds min over all contiguous partitions, which equals global min. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "cost of swapping assignments equal" if there are more than two orders? Repeated swaps. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "T_i" sorted and "order i" maybe not sorted by T? Constraints say T_1<=...<=T_N, so yes.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N<=100" and "K<=N" so O(N^3 K) maybe accepted. Our DP O(Σ states * K) with states O(N^2 per prefix) total O(N^3), transitions O(K N^3) maybe 1e8 worst but actual ~8M. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "date values T_p+cX" count per prefix O(i^2), so total states O(N^3). Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "dp[i]" maybe has fewer states but no problem.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "N=100" and "X=1e9" and "T_i=1e12" so dates all around 1e12; dict keys ints.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "pref" using 1-index; T list 0-index. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "input = sys.stdin.read().split()" and if no data; return. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N K X" maybe X up to 1e9, but NEG -1e30, base = d+X; if d is a real date and X large, base maybe >T_j. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "cost" values in dp maybe not minimal for a given date due to not pruning dominated states with same date? We update min. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "dp[j].get(nd, INF)" and if nd exists with value 0, old=0, nc<0? nc never negative, so no update. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "total dissatisfaction" maybe can be negative if T_i negative? No.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "At most K orders can be shipped together" and if a batch has size exactly K, next batch can start at date+X. Our transition from i to j with j-i=K, then next transition from j to l with l-j<=K. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "Once a shipment is made, the next shipment cannot be made until X days later" and if previous date d is less than T_i? In valid state, d>=T_i. But if not, transition still works; however dp shouldn't contain invalid. Let's verify dp0 NEG invalid but no orders. For i>0, when we create batch ending at j, nd>=T_j>=T_i for all i<=j, so valid. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "T_i" sorted but if T_j is huge, nd=T_j, and previous date d may be less than T_j; gap >X. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "shipment date" for a batch ending at j could be less than previous date+X if previous date is NEG? No, for first batch no previous. We handle NEG by max with T_j. For subsequent, nd=max(T_j,d+X)>=d+X. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "next shipment cannot be made until X days later" and if previous date d is NEG, d+X negative, but no previous shipment; max with T_j. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "Day a+X" if a is NEG, not a real day, but we don't use it as real because max with T_j. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "dp[0]" and "NEG" key causing date bound not apply; no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "min(dp[N].values())" if dp[N] has values but maybe some values are INF? no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "K" capacity and "N" small; no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "T_i" sorted but if there are duplicate T, the date candidate set T_p+cX maybe duplicates; dict handles.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "X" maybe 0? no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "At most K orders can be shipped together" and if a shipment can contain orders from non-contiguous positions after sorting by T but same shipment day; we argued contiguous.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "cost of swapping assignments equal" if the two orders are in shipments with different sizes? Swapping individual orders between shipments doesn't change shipment sizes, so capacity unchanged. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "order i can only be shipped on Day T_i or later" and if swapping earlier T order to earlier shipment and later T order to later shipment is feasible; we checked. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "total dissatisfaction" if an order is shipped in a batch with later orders, its dissatisfaction is counted until shipment; yes.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "Day a+X" if a is NEG, not a real day, but we don't use it as real because max with T_j. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "dp[0]" and "NEG" key causing date bound not apply; no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "min(dp[N].values())" if dp[N] has values but maybe some values are INF? no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "K" capacity and "N" small; no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "T_i" sorted but if there are duplicate T, the date candidate set T_p+cX maybe duplicates; dict handles.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "X" maybe 0? no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "At most K orders can be shipped together" and if a shipment can contain orders from non-contiguous positions after sorting by T but same shipment day; we argued contiguous.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "cost of swapping assignments equal" if the two orders are in shipments with different sizes? Swapping individual orders between shipments doesn't change shipment sizes, so capacity unchanged. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "order i can only be shipped on Day T_i or later" and if swapping earlier T order to earlier shipment and later T order to later shipment is feasible; we checked. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "total dissatisfaction" if an order is shipped in a batch with later orders, its dissatisfaction is counted until shipment; yes.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N=100" and "T_i" up to1e12, so answer fits in 64-bit? 1e14, yes. Python.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "pref" sum maybe 1e14, and cost add maybe (j-i)*nd - pref diff. If nd=1e12, size100, cost1e14 -1e14 =0 maybe. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "dp[i]" being a dict and iterating while updating dp[j] for j>i; no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "for i in range(N+1)" and if dp[i] gets updated from a previous i that is not processed yet? We process increasing, so all updates to dp[i] come from smaller indices, already processed. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "if not dp[i]: continue" and dp[0] has NEG so truthy; if a dp dict has key with value 0, truthy because dict nonempty. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "INF = 10**30" and if old = INF, and nc maybe INF? no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "NEG = -10**30" and if T_j can be 10^12, base negative; good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "X" up to1e9 and "NEG" -1e30, base = -999999999999999999999000000000000? Actually -10^30 + 10^9 = -999,999,999,999,999,999,999,000,000,000,000? Let's not care. It's <1.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "Day" starting at 1 and T_i>=1; no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "At most K orders can be shipped together" and if a batch size is less than K but there are more orders available; we can choose to ship fewer, leaving rest for later. DP allows j-i < K. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "Once a shipment is made, the next shipment cannot be made until X days later" and if we choose to wait longer than necessary before a shipment, could reduce future waiting? No, waiting longer only delays future shipments and increases current waiting. So no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "minimum possible total dissatisfaction" and if there is a trade-off between shipping a batch later to include more orders vs shipping earlier with fewer orders; DP explores all batch sizes.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "T_i" sorted and "order i" maybe not sorted by T if equal? no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "K" capacity and "batch size" in DP: We choose j-i orders in new batch. Since orders are contiguous, size = j-i. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "previous date d" in dp[i] maybe not the actual last shipment date if there were empty shipments? We don't use empty shipments. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "minimum possible total dissatisfaction" and if we can ship an order earlier than its placement by assigning it to a batch whose date is before T_i but the batch also contains later orders? No, impossible.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "Day 1, Day 2, Day 3, ..." and if T_i=1, shipping on Day0 not allowed. Our first batch date T_j>=1.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "NEG" and if T_j could be less than NEG+X? No.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "dp" list of dicts and using `if not dp[i]: continue`; dp[0] has key NEG so truthy. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "INF" and `old = dic.get(nd, INF)` if old is INF and nc maybe also INF? nc never INF. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "pref_i = pref[i]" and "pref[j]" in inner loop; list lookup. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "T[j-1]" in inner loop; list lookup. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "base = d + X" and if d is NEG, base negative; if X is 1e9, base still negative. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "max_j = min(N, i+K)" and if K=0? no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "range(i+1, max_j+1)" when i=N, range empty. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "print(min(dp[N].values()))" if dp[N] is empty due to bug; no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "T_i" sorted but if input has more numbers? no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "N K X" and "T_1 ... T_N" maybe on multiple lines; read split handles.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "At most K orders can be shipped together" and if a batch size can be zero to wait? No.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "Once a shipment is made, the next shipment cannot be made until X days later" and if we ship on Day a, can we ship on Day a+X+1? Yes, "can be made on Day a+X" means at least X days later. We use earliest.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimum possible total dissatisfaction" and if delaying a shipment could reduce dissatisfaction of future orders by allowing them to be in same batch? Wait, delaying a shipment to include future orders is represented by choosing a larger batch endpoint j. The shipment date for that batch is then max(T_j, prev+X), which may be later than if you shipped earlier without those future orders. The DP considers both options. For a fixed batch endpoint, earlier date is better. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "contiguous batches" and if a later order could be shipped earlier than an earlier order in an optimal schedule with equal cost; we can reorder without changing cost, so there exists contiguous optimal. DP finds min over all contiguous partitions, which equals global min. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "cost of swapping assignments equal" if there are more than two orders? Repeated swaps. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "T_i" sorted and "order i" maybe not sorted by T? Constraints say T_1<=...<=T_N, so yes.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N<=100" and "K<=N" so O(N^3 K) maybe accepted. Our DP O(Σ states * K) with states O(N^2 per prefix) total O(N^3), transitions O(K N^3) maybe 1e8 worst but actual ~8M. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "date values T_p+cX" count per prefix O(i^2), so total states O(N^3). Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "dp[i]" maybe has fewer states but no problem.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "N=100" and "X=1e9" and "T_i=1e12" so dates all around 1e12; dict keys ints.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "pref" using 1-index; T list 0-index. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "input = sys.stdin.read().split()" and if no data; return. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N K X" maybe X up to 1e9, but NEG -1e30, base = d+X; if d is a real date and X large, base maybe >T_j. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "cost" values in dp maybe not minimal for a given date due to not pruning dominated states with same date? We update min. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "dp[j].get(nd, INF)" and if nd exists with value 0, old=0, nc<0? nc never negative, so no update. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "total dissatisfaction" maybe can be negative if T_i negative? No.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "At most K orders can be shipped together" and if a batch has size exactly K, next batch can start at date+X. Our transition from i to j with j-i=K, then next transition from j to l with l-j<=K. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "Once a shipment is made, the next shipment cannot be made until X days later" and if previous date d is less than T_i? In valid state, d>=T_i. But if not, transition still works; however dp shouldn't contain invalid. Let's verify dp0 NEG invalid but no orders. For i>0, when we create batch ending at j, nd>=T_j>=T_i for all i<=j, so valid. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "N=100" and "X=1e9" and "T_i=1e12" so dates all around 1e12; dict keys ints.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "pref" using 1-index; T list 0-index. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "input = sys.stdin.read().split()" and if no data; return. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N K X" maybe X up to 1e9, but NEG -1e30, base = d+X; if d is a real date and X large, base maybe >T_j. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "cost" values in dp maybe not minimal for a given date due to not pruning dominated states with same date? We update min. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "dp[j].get(nd, INF)" and if nd exists with value 0, old=0, nc<0? nc never negative, so no update. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "total dissatisfaction" maybe can be negative if T_i negative? No.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "At most K orders can be shipped together" and if a batch has size exactly K, next batch can start at date+X. Our transition from i to j with j-i=K, then next transition from j to l with l-j<=K. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "Once a shipment is made, the next shipment cannot be made until X days later" and if previous date d is less than T_i? In valid state, d>=T_i. But if not, transition still works; however dp shouldn't contain invalid. Let's verify dp0 NEG invalid but no orders. For i>0, when we create batch ending at j, nd>=T_j>=T_i for all i<=j, so valid. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "T_i" sorted but if T_j is huge, nd=T_j, and previous date d may be less than T_j; gap >X. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "shipment date" for a batch ending at j could be less than previous date+X if previous date is NEG? No, for first batch no previous. We handle NEG by max with T_j. For subsequent, nd=max(T_j,d+X)>=d+X. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "next shipment cannot be made until X days later" and if previous date d is NEG, d+X negative, but no previous shipment; max with T_j. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "Day a+X" if a is NEG, not a real day, but we don't use it as real because max with T_j. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "dp[0]" and "NEG" key causing date bound not apply; no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "min(dp[N].values())" if dp[N] has values but maybe some values are INF? no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "K" capacity and "N" small; no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "T_i" sorted but if there are duplicate T, the date candidate set T_p+cX maybe duplicates; dict handles.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "X" maybe 0? no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "At most K orders can be shipped together" and if a shipment can contain orders from non-contiguous positions after sorting by T but same shipment day; we argued contiguous.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "cost of swapping assignments equal" if the two orders are in shipments with different sizes? Swapping individual orders between shipments doesn't change shipment sizes, so capacity unchanged. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "order i can only be shipped on Day T_i or later" and if swapping earlier T order to earlier shipment and later T order to later shipment is feasible; we checked. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "total dissatisfaction" if an order is shipped in a batch with later orders, its dissatisfaction is counted until shipment; yes.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N=100" and "T_i" up to1e12, so answer fits in 64-bit? 1e14, yes. Python.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "pref" sum maybe 1e14, and cost add maybe (j-i)*nd - pref diff. If nd=1e12, size100, cost1e14 -1e14 =0 maybe. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "dp[i]" being a dict and iterating while updating dp[j] for j>i; no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "for i in range(N+1)" and if dp[i] gets updated from a previous i that is not processed yet? We process increasing, so all updates to dp[i] come from smaller indices, already processed. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "if not dp[i]: continue" and dp[0] has NEG so truthy; if a dp dict has key with value 0, truthy because dict nonempty. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "INF = 10**30" and if old = INF, and nc maybe INF? no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "NEG = -10**30" and if T_j can be 10^12, base negative; good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "X" up to1e9 and "NEG" -1e30, base = -999999999999999999999000000000000? Actually -10^30 + 10^9 = -999,999,999,999,999,999,999,000,000,000,000? Let's not care. It's <1.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "Day" starting at 1 and T_i>=1; no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "At most K orders can be shipped together" and if a batch size is less than K but there are more orders available; we can choose to ship fewer, leaving rest for later. DP allows j-i < K. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "Once a shipment is made, the next shipment cannot be made until X days later" and if we choose to wait longer than necessary before a shipment, could reduce future waiting? No, waiting longer only delays future shipments and increases current waiting. So no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "minimum possible total dissatisfaction" and if there is a trade-off between shipping a batch later to include more orders vs shipping earlier with fewer orders; DP explores all batch sizes.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "T_i" sorted and "order i" maybe not sorted by T if equal? no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "K" capacity and "batch size" in DP: We choose j-i orders in new batch. Since orders are contiguous, size = j-i. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "previous date d" in dp[i] maybe not the actual last shipment date if there were empty shipments? We don't use empty shipments. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "minimum possible total dissatisfaction" and if we can ship an order earlier than its placement by assigning it to a batch whose date is before T_i but the batch also contains later orders? No, impossible.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "Day 1, Day 2, Day 3, ..." and if T_i=1, shipping on Day0 not allowed. Our first batch date T_j>=1.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "NEG" and if T_j could be less than NEG+X? No.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "dp" list of dicts and using `if not dp[i]: continue`; dp[0] has key NEG so truthy. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "INF" and `old = dic.get(nd, INF)` if old is INF and nc maybe also INF? nc never INF. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "pref_i = pref[i]" and "pref[j]" in inner loop; list lookup. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "T[j-1]" in inner loop; list lookup. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "base = d + X" and if d is NEG, base negative; if X is 1e9, base still negative. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "max_j = min(N, i+K)" and if K=0? no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "range(i+1, max_j+1)" when i=N, range empty. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "print(min(dp[N].values()))" if dp[N] is empty due to bug; no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "T_i" sorted but if input has more numbers? no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "N K X" and "T_1 ... T_N" maybe on multiple lines; read split handles.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "At most K orders can be shipped together" and if a batch size can be zero to wait? No.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "Once a shipment is made, the next shipment cannot be made until X days later" and if we ship on Day a, can we ship on Day a+X+1? Yes, "can be made on Day a+X" means at least X days later. We use earliest.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimum possible total dissatisfaction" and if delaying a shipment could reduce dissatisfaction of future orders by allowing them to be in same batch? Wait, delaying a shipment to include future orders is represented by choosing a larger batch endpoint j. The shipment date for that batch is then max(T_j, prev+X), which may be later than if you shipped earlier without those future orders. The DP considers both options. For a fixed batch endpoint, earlier date is better. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "contiguous batches" and if a later order could be shipped earlier than an earlier order in an optimal schedule with equal cost; we can reorder without changing cost, so there exists contiguous optimal. DP finds min over all contiguous partitions, which equals global min. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "cost of swapping assignments equal" if there are more than two orders? Repeated swaps. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "T_i" sorted and "order i" maybe not sorted by T? Constraints say T_1<=...<=T_N, so yes.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N<=100" and "K<=N" so O(N^3 K) maybe accepted. Our DP O(Σ states * K) with states O(N^2 per prefix) total O(N^3), transitions O(K N^3) maybe 1e8 worst but actual ~8M. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "T_i" sorted but if input has more numbers? no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "N K X" and "T_1 ... T_N" maybe on multiple lines; read split handles.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "At most K orders can be shipped together" and if a batch size can be zero to wait? No.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "Once a shipment is made, the next shipment cannot be made until X days later" and if we ship on Day a, can we ship on Day a+X+1? Yes, "can be made on Day a+X" means at least X days later. We use earliest.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimum possible total dissatisfaction" and if delaying a shipment could reduce dissatisfaction of future orders by allowing them to be in same batch? Wait, delaying a shipment to include future orders is represented by choosing a larger batch endpoint j. The shipment date for that batch is then max(T_j, prev+X), which may be later than if you shipped earlier without those future orders. The DP considers both options. For a fixed batch endpoint, earlier date is better. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "contiguous batches" and if a later order could be shipped earlier than an earlier order in an optimal schedule with equal cost; we can reorder without changing cost, so there exists contiguous optimal. DP finds min over all contiguous partitions, which equals global min. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "cost of swapping assignments equal" if there are more than two orders? Repeated swaps. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "T_i" sorted and "order i" maybe not sorted by T? Constraints say T_1<=...<=T_N, so yes.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N<=100" and "K<=N" so O(N^3 K) maybe accepted. Our DP O(Σ states * K) with states O(N^2 per prefix) total O(N^3), transitions O(K N^3) maybe 1e8 worst but actual ~8M. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "date values T_p+cX" count per prefix O(i^2), so total states O(N^3). Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "dp[i]" maybe has fewer states but no problem.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "N=100" and "X=1e9" and "T_i=1e12" so dates all around 1e12; dict keys ints.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "pref" using 1-index; T list 0-index. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "input = sys.stdin.read().split()" and if no data; return. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N K X" maybe X up to 1e9, but NEG -1e30, base = d+X; if d is a real date and X large, base maybe >T_j. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "cost" values in dp maybe not minimal for a given date due to not pruning dominated states with same date? We update min. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "dp[j].get(nd, INF)" and if nd exists with value 0, old=0, nc<0? nc never negative, so no update. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "total dissatisfaction" maybe can be negative if T_i negative? No.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "At most K orders can be shipped together" and if a batch has size exactly K, next batch can start at date+X. Our transition from i to j with j-i=K, then next transition from j to l with l-j<=K. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "Once a shipment is made, the next shipment cannot be made until X days later" and if previous date d is less than T_i? In valid state, d>=T_i. But if not, transition still works; however dp shouldn't contain invalid. Let's verify dp0 NEG invalid but no orders. For i>0, when we create batch ending at j, nd>=T_j>=T_i for all i<=j, so valid. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "T_i" sorted but if T_j is huge, nd=T_j, and previous date d may be less than T_j; gap >X. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "shipment date" for a batch ending at j could be less than previous date+X if previous date is NEG? No, for first batch no previous. We handle NEG by max with T_j. For subsequent, nd=max(T_j,d+X)>=d+X. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "next shipment cannot be made until X days later" and if previous date d is NEG, d+X negative, but no previous shipment; max with T_j. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "Day a+X" if a is NEG, not a real day, but we don't use it as real because max with T_j. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "dp[0]" and "NEG" key causing date bound not apply; no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "min(dp[N].values())" if dp[N] has values but maybe some values are INF? no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "K" capacity and "N" small; no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "T_i" sorted but if there are duplicate T, the date candidate set T_p+cX maybe duplicates; dict handles.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "X" maybe 0? no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "At most K orders can be shipped together" and if a shipment can contain orders from non-contiguous positions after sorting by T but same shipment day; we argued contiguous.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "cost of swapping assignments equal" if the two orders are in shipments with different sizes? Swapping individual orders between shipments doesn't change shipment sizes, so capacity unchanged. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "order i can only be shipped on Day T_i or later" and if swapping earlier T order to earlier shipment and later T order to later shipment is feasible; we checked. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "total dissatisfaction" if an order is shipped in a batch with later orders, its dissatisfaction is counted until shipment; yes.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N=100" and "T_i" up to1e12, so answer fits in 64-bit? 1e14, yes. Python.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "pref" sum maybe 1e14, and cost add maybe (j-i)*nd - pref diff. If nd=1e12, size100, cost1e14 -1e14 =0 maybe. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "dp[i]" being a dict and iterating while updating dp[j] for j>i; no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "for i in range(N+1)" and if dp[i] gets updated from a previous i that is not processed yet? We process increasing, so all updates to dp[i] come from smaller indices, already processed. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "if not dp[i]: continue" and dp[0] has NEG so truthy; if a dp dict has key with value 0, truthy because dict nonempty. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "INF = 10**30" and if old = INF, and nc maybe INF? no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "NEG = -10**30" and if T_j can be 10^12, base negative; good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "X" up to1e9 and "NEG" -1e30, base = -999999999999999999999000000000000? Actually -10^30 + 10^9 = -999,999,999,999,999,999,999,000,000,000,000? Let's not care. It's <1.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "Day" starting at 1 and T_i>=1; no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "At most K orders can be shipped together" and if a batch size is less than K but there are more orders available; we can choose to ship fewer, leaving rest for later. DP allows j-i < K. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "Once a shipment is made, the next shipment cannot be made until X days later" and if we choose to wait longer than necessary before a shipment, could reduce future waiting? No, waiting longer only delays future shipments and increases current waiting. So no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "minimum possible total dissatisfaction" and if there is a trade-off between shipping a batch later to include more orders vs shipping earlier with fewer orders; DP explores all batch sizes.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "T_i" sorted and "order i" maybe not sorted by T if equal? no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "K" capacity and "batch size" in DP: We choose j-i orders in new batch. Since orders are contiguous, size = j-i. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "previous date d" in dp[i] maybe not the actual last shipment date if there were empty shipments? We don't use empty shipments. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "minimum possible total dissatisfaction" and if we can ship an order earlier than its placement by assigning it to a batch whose date is before T_i but the batch also contains later orders? No, impossible.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "Day 1, Day 2, Day 3, ..." and if T_i=1, shipping on Day0 not allowed. Our first batch date T_j>=1.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "NEG" and if T_j could be less than NEG+X? No.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "INF" and `old = dic.get(nd, INF)` if old is INF and nc maybe also INF? nc never INF. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "T_i" sorted and "order i" maybe not sorted by T if equal? no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "K" capacity and "batch size" in DP: We choose j-i orders in new batch. Since orders are contiguous, size = j-i. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "previous date d" in dp[i] maybe not the actual last shipment date if there were empty shipments? We don't use empty shipments. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "minimum possible total dissatisfaction" and if we can ship an order earlier than its placement by assigning it to a batch whose date is before T_i but the batch also contains later orders? No, impossible.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "Day 1, Day 2, Day 3, ..." and if T_i=1, shipping on Day0 not allowed. Our first batch date T_j>=1.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "NEG" and if T_j could be less than NEG+X? No.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "dp" list of dicts and using `if not dp[i]: continue`; dp[0] has key NEG so truthy. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "INF" and `old = dic.get(nd, INF)` if old is INF and nc maybe also INF? nc never INF. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "pref_i = pref[i]" and "pref[j]" in inner loop; list lookup. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "T[j-1]" in inner loop; list lookup. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "base = d + X" and if d is NEG, base negative; if X is 1e9, base still negative. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "max_j = min(N, i+K)" and if K=0? no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "range(i+1, max_j+1)" when i=N, range empty. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "print(min(dp[N].values()))" if dp[N] is empty due to bug; no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "T_i" sorted but if input has more numbers? no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "N K X" and "T_1 ... T_N" maybe on multiple lines; read split handles.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "At most K orders can be shipped together" and if a batch size can be zero to wait? No.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "Once a shipment is made, the next shipment cannot be made until X days later" and if we ship on Day a, can we ship on Day a+X+1? Yes, "can be made on Day a+X" means at least X days later. We use earliest.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimum possible total dissatisfaction" and if delaying a shipment could reduce dissatisfaction of future orders by allowing them to be in same batch? Wait, delaying a shipment to include future orders is represented by choosing a larger batch endpoint j. The shipment date for that batch is then max(T_j, prev+X), which may be later than if you shipped earlier without those future orders. The DP considers both options. For a fixed batch endpoint, earlier date is better. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "contiguous batches" and if a later order could be shipped earlier than an earlier order in an optimal schedule with equal cost; we can reorder without changing cost, so there exists contiguous optimal. DP finds min over all contiguous partitions, which equals global min. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "cost of swapping assignments equal" if there are more than two orders? Repeated swaps. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "T_i" sorted and "order i" maybe not sorted by T? Constraints say T_1<=...<=T_N, so yes.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N<=100" and "K<=N" so O(N^3 K) maybe accepted. Our DP O(Σ states * K) with states O(N^2 per prefix) total O(N^3), transitions O(K N^3) maybe 1e8 worst but actual ~8M. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "date values T_p+cX" count per prefix O(i^2), so total states O(N^3). Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "dp[i]" maybe has fewer states but no problem.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "N=100" and "X=1e9" and "T_i=1e12" so dates all around 1e12; dict keys ints.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "pref" using 1-index; T list 0-index. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "input = sys.stdin.read().split()" and if no data; return. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N K X" maybe X up to 1e9, but NEG -1e30, base = d+X; if d is a real date and X large, base maybe >T_j. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "cost" values in dp maybe not minimal for a given date due to not pruning dominated states with same date? We update min. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "dp[j].get(nd, INF)" and if nd exists with value 0, old=0, nc<0? nc never negative, so no update. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "total dissatisfaction" maybe can be negative if T_i negative? No.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "At most K orders can be shipped together" and if a batch has size exactly K, next batch can start at date+X. Our transition from i to j with j-i=K, then next transition from j to l with l-j<=K. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "Once a shipment is made, the next shipment cannot be made until X days later" and if previous date d is less than T_i? In valid state, d>=T_i. But if not, transition still works; however dp shouldn't contain invalid. Let's verify dp0 NEG invalid but no orders. For i>0, when we create batch ending at j, nd>=T_j>=T_i for all i<=j, so valid. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "T_i" sorted but if T_j is huge, nd=T_j, and previous date d may be less than T_j; gap >X. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "shipment date" for a batch ending at j could be less than previous date+X if previous date is NEG? No, for first batch no previous. We handle NEG by max with T_j. For subsequent, nd=max(T_j,d+X)>=d+X. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "next shipment cannot be made until X days later" and if previous date d is NEG, d+X negative, but no previous shipment; max with T_j. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "Day a+X" if a is NEG, not a real day, but we don't use it as real because max with T_j. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "dp[0]" and "NEG" key causing date bound not apply; no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "min(dp[N].values())" if dp[N] has values but maybe some values are INF? no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "K" capacity and "N" small; no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "T_i" sorted but if there are duplicate T, the date candidate set T_p+cX maybe duplicates; dict handles.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "X" maybe 0? no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "At most K orders can be shipped together" and if a shipment can contain orders from non-contiguous positions after sorting by T but same shipment day; we argued contiguous.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "cost of swapping assignments equal" if the two orders are in shipments with different sizes? Swapping individual orders between shipments doesn't change shipment sizes, so capacity unchanged. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "order i can only be shipped on Day T_i or later" and if swapping earlier T order to earlier shipment and later T order to later shipment is feasible; we checked. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "total dissatisfaction" if an order is shipped in a batch with later orders, its dissatisfaction is counted until shipment; yes.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N=100" and "T_i" up to1e12, so answer fits in 64-bit? 1e14, yes. Python.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "pref" sum maybe 1e14, and cost add maybe (j-i)*nd - pref diff. If nd=1e12, size100, cost1e14 -1e14 =0 maybe. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "dp[i]" being a dict and iterating while updating dp[j] for j>i; no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "for i in range(N+1)" and if dp[i] gets updated from a previous i that is not processed yet? We process increasing, so all updates to dp[i] come from smaller indices, already processed. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "if not dp[i]: continue" and dp[0] has NEG so truthy; if a dp dict has key with value 0, truthy because dict nonempty. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "INF = 10**30" and if old = INF, and nc maybe INF? no.
